      "raw_content": "[Luxe Adventure Traveler](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/)\n\nAdventure Travel With a Glass of Wine\n\nYou are here: [Home](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/) / [Europe](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/category/europe/) / [France](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/category/europe/france/) / A Local’s Take on the Best Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\n# A Local’s Take on the Best Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\nBy [Jennifer Dombrowski](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/author/jdombrowski/) [60 Comments](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/best-restaurants-in-bordeaux/#comments)\n\nBordeaux’s revitalization is more than new tourism initiatives, the sprawling [La Cité du Vin](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/la-cite-du-vin-bordeaux/) and a city that has been awakened like a shiny new diamond emerging from under the black soot. The city long had a reputation for a sub par food scene; one that certainly didn’t match with top notch wines in the Wine Capital of the World. The best restaurants in Bordeaux were few and far between. But that has all changed, along with so many other things, and Bordeaux now offers one of the best foodie scenes in France on par with [Lyon](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/things-not-to-miss-in-lyon/) and [Paris](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/best-restaurants-in-paris-france/).\n\nSo much of the [culture in the Port of the Moon](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/top-things-to-do-in-bordeaux-france/) is about enjoying good food and good wine. Not only have some of the biggest names in the chefdom added Bordeaux to their always lengthening lists of restaurants, hot new chefs are continually emerging. But like anywhere, it’s definitely possible to find some duds and can be difficult to choose where to eat in Bordeaux from the hundreds of restaurants, bistros and brasseries seemingly gracing every corner and lining every square. To help with that trip planning problem, we put together our local’s take on the best restaurants in Bordeaux.\n\nOne thing you’ll notice is that we mention the menu changes seasonally at nearly all of our favorite restaurants. A seasonally changing menu is the hallmark of a good restaurant and something locals look for to indicate locally sourced and quality ingredients. And if you need other French dining out etiquette tips, check out our guide to the [must-know rules for eating out in France](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/french-dining-etiquette-8-rules-for-dining-out-in-france/) that will help you fit in like a local.\n\nWe highly recommend making a reservation as it’s typical that restaurants in France will only have one seating and once the tables are full, you’ll be turned away. We love using LaFourchette to make reservations. The website and app takes any language barriers out of making restaurant reservations in Bordeaux with an easy to use system to reserve your table in just a few clicks. Popular in Bordeaux and many other cities in France, LaFourchette includes sample menus, photos, directions and one-click reservation booking for many restaurants. Download [TheFork app](https://www.thefork.fr/) or use the links below to book your reservations.\n\n[Bordelais & French](#Bordelaise) | [Seafood](#Seafood) | [Michelin Star](#Michelin) | [Quick Bites](#Quick) | [Pizza & Italian](#Pizza) | [Burgers](#Burgers) | [Great International Restaurants](#International) | [Asian](#Asian) | [Food Halls](#FoodHallsBDX) | [Wine & Tapas Bars](#WineBarsBDX) | [Sunday Brunch](#BrunchBDX) | [Ptisserie](#Patisserie)   \n  \n \n\n## For the Best Bordelais and French Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\n[![Le Brasserie Bordelaise](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventiure-Traveler-Bordeaux-Le-Brasserie-Bordelaise-1.jpg)\n\nA veal chop and steak topped with foie gras accompanied by frites and mashed potatoes\n\n### La Brasserie Bordelaise\n\nLocated on Rue Saint-Rémi, one of the busiest streets in Bordeaux, La Brasserie Bordelaise is a gem among many restaurants that cater to tourists. Locals will even elbow their way through the crowds on Rue Saint-Remi for a reservation at this Bordeaux restaurant.\n\nIt’s all about the meat at La Brasserie Bordelaise and this is where you come when you want a good steak or French comfort food typical of the Southwest where French meets Basque. The menu does change seasonally, like in winter when the famous black truffles of the Perigord grace the mashed potatoes or flavor a penne and foie gras. And the steaks are seriously to die for. They’re so tender they melt in your mouth and only breeds like boeuf de Bazas, the Blonde d’Aquitaine and Simmental are good enough to grace this menu.\n\nThe wine list is extensive, with over 700 bottles to choose from. In fact, the wine is coming out of the woodwork with bottles stacked in every nook and cranny. Wander around the various rooms and wine cellar to have a look.\n\n*[La Brasserie Bordelaise](https://www.brasserie-bordelaise.fr/), 50 Rue Saint-Rémi. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n![The sliced steak served in the secret sauce with fries and wine at L'Entrecôte Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nSteak frites lovers look no further than L’Entrecôte when visiting Bordeaux\n\n### L’Entrecôte\n\nIf you’re looking the French classic steak frites, there’s simply no better place to head to in Bordeaux than L’Entrecôte.\n\nLocated right in the heart of Bordeaux steps away from the Grand Thétre, L’Entrecôte’s menu features one thing and one thing only: 170 grams of sliced beef sirloin served with the restaurant’s famous secret sauce and as many fries as you can eat. It comes with a simple green salad with walnuts as a starter. Your only choice is how you’d like the steak cooked. There’s even only one choice if you’d like red wine to go with it all. If you need a little something sweet to finish off the savory meal, there is a small menu of dessert options.\n\nThe formula has been unchanged since 1962 since founder Henri Gineste opened the first location of his now popular restaurant in Toulouse. And since, the well-known restaurant has opened locations here in Bordeaux, Nantes, Montpellier, Lyon and even Barcelona.\n\nThe restaurant doesn’t accept reservations, but locals and visitors alike line up come both lunch and dinnertime to snag one of coveted tables. And we’ve been among that line many times. It truly is that good and worth the wait.\n\nThere’s two dinner services, and as L’Entrecôte quite literally specializes in one thing, the services are a well-oiled machine. We recommend arriving at least 30 minutes prior to service to ensure you’ll get a table.\n\n*[L’Entrecôte](https://www.entrecote.fr/), 4 Cours du 30 Juillet. Reservations not accepted. Arrive at least 30 minutes prior to service. Dog-friendly, but note that space is particularly tight between tables.*\n\n[![La Tupina Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20562'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Bordeaux-La-Tupina-1.jpg)\n\nStuffed canelé with salted caramel\n\n### La Tupina\n\nLa Tupina is a Bordeaux institution and rightfully so. The restaurant is comprised of several rooms, all with rustic vintage furniture giving is a French farmhouse feel. The food is prepared in an open fire pit and in winter, you’ll find soups simmering in the cauldron hanging in the fireplace.\n\nIt’s pricey for a meal at La Tupina, but a few bites of the Southwestern France specialties like foie gras terrine and potatoes cooked in duck fat make it oh-so-worth it. Be sure to order dessert too. Bordeaux’s famous little custardy cake, the canelé, typically is on their dessert menu and its deliciously served “stuffed” with homemade ice cream and drizzled with salted caramel.\n\n*[La Tupina](https://latupina.com/), 6 Rue Porte de la Monnaie. Reservations required. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/la-tupina-r184).*\n\n[![Le 7 Restaurant Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20489'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/08/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Le-7-Restaurant-4.jpg)\n\nFilet of beef with mashed potatoes and root vegetables\n\n### Le 7 Restaurant\n\nLe 7 Restaurant is on the 7th floor of [La Cité du Vin](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/la-cite-du-vin-bordeaux/) and offers a stunning panoramic view of Bordeaux and the Garonne River. I recommend booking a table about an hour before sunset when dining in the summer so that you can watch the sky turn shades of pink and lavender over fine wine and Bordelaise fare.\n\nThe menu features seasonal dishes and an impressive wine list, which you can browse by country on an iPad. We also love their pairing suggestions menu featuring small plates with selections from around the world like salmon from Scotland and tuna from Japan.\n\n*[Le 7 Restaurant](http://www.le7restaurant.com/en/), 4 Rue de Pontac. Reservations recommended. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n[![Le Chien de Pavlov Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Bordeaux-Le-Chien-de-Pavlov-1.jpg)\n\nFilet with polenta and Bordelaise sauce\n\n### Le Chien de Pavlov\n\nI love the ambiance of Le Chien de Pavlov. Expect mismatched antique furniture and tiny Tiffany lights in the cozy restaurant. The menu changes depending on what ingredients are fresh and available seasonally. But one thing you can be assured of is that this restaurant in the heart of Bordeaux’s Saint Pierre district is excellent value.\n\nActually, I like to tell people that Le Chien de Pavlov offers the best value glass of wine in Bordeaux. With an entree, a glass of the white or red on offer for the day is just €2. And the food is delicious too.\n\n*[Le Chien de Pavlov](http://www.lechiendepavlov.com/), 45 Rue de la Devise. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n[![Roasted lamb dressed with mustard and accompanied by mushrooms and bok choy at Symbiose](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Symbiose-Bordeaux-1.jpg)\n\nRoasted lamb dressed with mustard and accompanied by mushrooms and bok choy at Symbiose\n\n### Symbiose\n\nSymbiose is a restaurant/cocktail bar that is incredibly difficult to get a table at. The brainchild of four friends, it’s Lucas, Simon and Thomas that are in charge of the pickling and the bar menu. While Félix is at the helm of their much-talked-about cuisine.\n\nIt’s only open as a restaurant Monday – Saturday for lunch and on Tuesdays -Saturday for dinner. The rest of the time it’s a speakeasy known for their craft cocktails – and the vibe entirely changes. If you dine at one of the dozen hard-to-reserve tables, take note of the grandfather clock at the back of the restaurant. It has a secret…opening up to an entirely hidden room where the nightly speakeasy is a Bordeaux hot spot.\n\nAs for the food, the rumors circulating Bordeaux are true and it’s exquisite. The lunch menu is a particularly good value, too, for a high quality gastronomic restaurant. You have a menu for €20 that includes a starter, main and dessert. As usual with the restaurants we seek out, the menu changes often and is based on local, fresh seasonal ingredients. It has just two choices each for the starter, main and dessert.\n\nOn the day we dined at Symbiose, we started with a marinated langoustine with asparagus and carrot and citrus purées that was topped with tuile (a type of French wafer) made of squid ink. Roasted lamb with bok choy, mushrooms and parsnip purée followed as the main course. And dessert was an oh-so-delicious chocolate whisky tart topped with salted caramel butter that I’ll be dreaming of for some time to come.\n\n*[Symbiose](https://symbiose-bordeaux.com/), 4 Quai des Chartrons. Reservations required. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n![Veal stuffed with mushrooms on a bed of mashed potatoes and served in a cast iron pan at OST Restaurant](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nVeal stuffed with mushrooms on a bed of mashed potatoes at OST\n\n### OST Restaurant\n\n[OST Restaurant](https://bordeauxtravelguide.com/ost-restaurant-bordeaux/) is a bistronomique restaurant located steps away from the Place du Palais in Bordeaux’s historic Quartier Saint Pierre. The restaurant menu is contemporary French with Asian flare inspired by the owners’ time living in Hong Kong.\n\nThe menu changes regularly to highlight the fresh, seasonal ingredients of the South West of France with unique Asian notes like a pinch of bird’s eye chili or ponzu. In theme with the friendly atmosphere, main courses are meant to be ordered and the meal shared family-style much as you would in many Asian cultures.\n\nOST is an impeccable experience to be share among friends or even as a date night. Between the generous cuisine and warm atmosphere, you’ll enjoy a relaxed meal of good food and leave feeling completely satisfied.\n\nDon’t miss trying OST’s signature dessert: their chocolate cookie with kalamata olives. It’s the definition of the perfect salty-sweet bite.\n\n*[OST Restaurant](https://www.ostbordeaux.com/), 8 Rue Ausone. Reservations required. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n![Duck cooked in the wood fireplace and carved into breast and legs at Brasserie Vinatier](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nDuck cooked in the wood fireplace\n\n### Brasserie Vinatier\n\n[Brasserie Vinatier](https://bordeauxtravelguide.com/brasserie-vinatier-bordeaux/) is the latest in a growing list of successful restaurants across France by the group La Nouvelle Garde, serving fresh, locally produced dishes at fair prices. Located just steps away from Place Pey-Berland in the very heart of Bordeaux, the brasserie offers a menu of both French classics you’ll find in the chicest Parisian brasserie and re-imagined specialties of the Sud Ouest.\n\nThe heart of Brasserie Vinatier is its large open kitchen and massive wood burning fireplace, where many of the dishes are cooked. Just about every table has a view to watch the chef waltz as everything from lionhead bowls of soupe à l’oignon gratinée with melted cheese clinging to the sides to steak with béarnaise sauce and heaps of homemade fries are prepared.\n\nEverything at Brasserie Vinatier is made in house, and the menu does change to reflect the best produce and products of the season.\n\n*[Brasserie Vinatier,](https://lanouvellegarde.com/fr/brasserie-vinatier/)* *3 Place Saint-Christoly. Dog-friendly. Reservations highly recommended. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n![Pork tenderloin with garlic and thyme mashed potatoes, snap peas and fennel at Mets Mots](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nPork tenderloin with garlic and thyme mashed potatoes, snap peas and fennel\n\n### Mets Mots\n\nLocated just a short walk from Jardin Public in the Fondaudège district, Mets Mots is a locally loved lunch spot. Awarded a BIB Gourmand by the Michelin Guide, a special distinction that recognizes restaurants serving high quality food for great value, Mets Mots offers a “brewery” menu for lunch Monday – Friday. While you have a choice between just two starters, two main courses, and two desserts, the menu changes weekly to highlight seasonal ingredients.\n\nIn the evenings, Mets Mots is a wine bar and serves a selection of small plates meant to be shared among friends.\n\nMets Mots has both a commitment to being eco-friendly and sourcing ingredients from local producers.\n\n*[Mets Mots](https://www.metsmots.fr/), 98 Rue Fondaudège*. *Dog-friendly. Reservations highly recommended. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n![24 hour cooked roast suckling lamb and veal roast at Chez Dupont](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20562'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\n24 hour cooked roast suckling lamb and veal roast at Chez Dupont\n\n### Chez Dupont\n\nChez Dupont is a Bordeaux institution since 1988. Located on the beautiful Rue Notre Dame in the Chartrons district, this restaurant is beloved by locals and tourists alike.\n\nChez Dupont is distinguished by its’ exceptional menu featuring seasonal fresh ingredients and products rigorously chosen by the chefs, who make everything in house. At lunchtime, a menu offers diners a choice from three starters, three main courses, and three desserts. Of course, the a la carte menu is also available at both lunch and dinner services.\n\nA personal favorite in the Chatrons district, I always love some of Chez Dupont’s menu staples like the veal rump roast, octopus (typically available in summer), scallops (available in winter), and the white asparagus from Blaye (available in April and May).\n\n*[Chez Dupont](https://www.chez-dupont.com/), 45 Rue Notre Dame. Dog-friendly. Reservations highly recommended. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n[![Le Canopee Cafe](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20500'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Le-Canopee-Cafe-1.jpg)\n\nShrimp tartare in an open ravioli in a shellfish broth and yuzu infusion\n\n### Le Canopée Café\n\nLe Canopée Café is a rooftop restaurant located in Mérignac, just 20 minutes by car from the center of Bordeaux or 3o minutes by the Line 1 bus. And it’s definitely worth the short journey out of town.\n\nThe restaurant has a lunch service, which can be seated indoors in their chic restaurant space with an open kitchen where you can see the chef and staff preparing the meals, or on the outdoor terrace when the weather is nice. In the summer evenings, there’s also the option of the outdoor tapas bar where there is a selection of small plates, wine and cocktails and several pétanque playing areas (pétanque is a French game sort of similar to Italian bocce, where you throw hollow steel balls as close as possible to a jack).\n\nWe definitely want to go back with some friends one evening for France’s answer to a pool bar and pub in the US. For now, we can highly recommend the restaurant for either lunch or dinner. We sampled a variety of Le Canopée Café’s dishes and all were artfully crafted dishes full of flavor that play on the local Bordelaise cuisine with a modern twist.\n\nWe opted for our waiter’s recommendations, since the entire menu looked enticing. We tried the shrimp tartare in a yuzu cream and a poached egg with ham. As our main courses, we tried their signature dish (and one of the owner’s favorites), a squid ink spaghetti carbonara topped with calamari as our starters. We also had the veal with potatoes. The meal was finished off with a little plate of chocolate bliss with six different chocolate creations from the pastry chef.\n\n*[Le Canopée Café](https://www.canopee-cafe.fr/), 1 Chemin de Pouchon, Mérignac. Reservations recommended. Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n[![Lamb shank with sweet potato puree at Bistro du Musee](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Bistro-du-Musee-Bordeaux-1.jpg)\n\nLamb shank with sweet potato puree at Bistro du Musée\n\n### Bistro du Musée\n\nBistro du Musée is located just across from Cathédrale Saint-André, one of the main sights of Bordeaux. While we would usually recommend you steer clear of restaurants in the vicinity of a major tourist attraction, Bistro du Musée is a temple of wine with over 50 bottle choices and many on offer by the glass. The bistro also offers a menu of local dishes that changes regularly with what’s in season.\n\nWe’ve eaten a lot of lamb shanks over the years and Tim declared the seven-hour roasted lamb shank the best he’s ever had. Our friends who we dined with for lunch also ordered the lamb shank and nodded in agreement between bites. Between the cozy atmosphere decorated with a wine atmosphere and the delicious, homemade food, you can bet we’ll be back. And next time, there’s no doubt that I’ll also be ordering the lamb shank.\n\n*[Bistro du Musée](https://www.lebistrodumusee.com/), 37 Place Pey Berland. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/le-bistro-du-musee-r7298).*\n\n[![Leg of lamb sliced at Le Chiopot](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20562'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Le-Chiopot-Bordeaux-1.jpg)\n\nLe Chiopot’s leg of lamb is meant to shared with at least two people\n\n### Le Chiopot\n\nLe Chiopot has been a Bordeaux institution for more than 75 years. The restaurant, which is actually located just a 15-minute taxi ride from Bordeaux Center, was located close to the slaughterhouses supplying meat to Bordeaux when it opened in 1927. Le Chiopot, not surprisingly because of the location, became well-known as one of the best spots in Bordeaux for meat dishes.\n\nThe decor has been modernized, but the menu remains meat-centric and still offers some of the best in Bordeaux. Bring friends, because menu items like the 1.5 kilograms of prime rib or the leg of lamb from the Pyrenees that we had are meant to be shared among at least 2-3 people. Believe us, it’s well worth the taxi ride for a meal at Le Chiopot.\n\n*[Le Chiopot](http://www.lechiopot.fr/), 281 Allée des Quatre Castéra, Bègles. Reservations recommended. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/le-chiopot-r9754).*\n\n[![Beef cheek on a bed of pumpkin puree at Restaurant LouLou](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Restaurant-LouLou-Bordeaux-1.jpg)\n\nBeef cheek on a bed of pumpkin puree at Restaurant LouLou\n\n### Restaurant LouLou\n\nLouLou is still relatively new on the Bordeaux scene. It’s located just steps away from Place de la Bourse and the interior is everything you expect of a French bistro. It’s cozy and has a gorgeous fireplace. You can definitely imagine you’re in France, even when dining indoors – which we think is sometimes lost in restaurants that are decorated with modern decor.\n\nAs you’ve probably come to realize, we like when there’s a chalkboard menu that changes with what’s fresh and in season. It’s a great indicator of a good restaurant. And that’s exactly what LouLou’s menu is – a chalkboard menu of the day. Though, there seem to be a few staples like the beef tataki, that simply change their accompaniments with what’s in season.\n\nThe beef tataki is the French version, not the Japanese. It’s beef cheek that is so incredibly tender it melts in your mouth. On an early winter day, it was accompanied by pumpkin puree and snap peas.\n\n*[Restaurant LouLou](https://louloubordeaux.com/), 16 Rue de la Cour des Aides. Dog-friendly. Reservations recommended.* *Book your reservation online on their website.*\n\n![Lamb with potato puree, roasted onion, carrot and celery at Arcada](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20562'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nLamb with potato puree, roasted onion, carrot and celery at Arcada\n\n### Arcada\n\nNestled in the heart of Old Bordeaux and tucked away on a tiny street just a few minutes from the Porte Cailhau, Arcada is a fine-dining address in Bordeaux open since May 2019. At the helm is the young, but experienced chef/owner Sofiane Bouhabib. He’s worked in several other Bordeaux restaurants we recommend, as well as in Biarritz and Bayonne.\n\nThe menu changes regularly to reflect the best seasonal ingredients from the region, and small producers from around the original supply the ingredients to the restaurant.\n\nThe restaurant has an impressive wine cellar with some 50 references that reflect not only the best of France, but also wine regions from around the world.\n\nAnd if you’re a cigar lover, you’re in for a treat. Ask Chef Bouhabib about his cigar collection.\n\n*[Arcada](https://www.arcada-restaurant.fr/), 13 Rue de la Rousselle. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/arcada-r497221).*\n\n## For the Best Seafood in Bordeaux\n\n![A pot of steaming mussels in curry sauce with a side of fries and wine at Les Moules du Cabanon](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nLes Moules du Cabanon specializes in moules frites\n\n### Les Moules du Cabanon\n\nWhile moules frites is a fairly popular dish across France, it’s actually not too common here in Bordeaux. Mussels are an invasive species for oyster farmers in the Bassin of Arcachon. So while oysters appear on menus all over the city, moules frites are a little harder to find.\n\nMussels fans should head to Les Moules du Cabanon on the quay. As the name implies, this restaurant specializes in gigantic pots of steaming mussels – and at a very reasonable price, too. The formula comes with a salad, home made fries and your choice from nine different sauces: marinières, Roquefort, cream, maraichères, curry, Ardennaise (mushroom, shallots, bacon, white wine and cream), Provençale (black olive, eggplant and peppers in a tomato and herbs from Provence sauce), Basquaise (chorizo, Espelette pepper and peppers in a tomato garlic sauce) or gratinées.\n\nWe highly recommend the curry and Basquaise, which are our go-to favorites. We order one of each and trade half way through for a bit of each. Pair the moules frites with a bottle of Entre-Deux-Mers white wine from Bordeaux’s Right Bank.\n\n*[Les Moules du Cabanon](https://lesmoulesducabanon.fr/), 12 Quai Richelieu. Dog-friendly. Reservations recommended.*\n\n## For the Best Michelin Starred Restaurants in Bordeaux and Fine Dining Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\n[![Le Pressoir d'Argent Gordon Ramsay](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/08/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Intercontintental-Bordeaux-Pressoir-d-Argent-Gordon-Ramsay-1.jpg)\n\nA soft boiled egg in a rich sautéed wild mushroom cream and Jerusalem artichoke\n\n### Le Pressoir d’Argent Gordon Ramsay\n\nWe’ve yet to be disappointed by a Gordon Ramsay restaurant and you just know any restaurant under the infamous Hell’s Kitchen chef is worth the splurge. The menu changes seasonally, but you can expect local products from the Aquitaine like beef de Baza, a local breed of beef that is basically the wagyu beef of France and oysters from Cap Ferret. Le Pressoir d’Argent is named after their rare silver lobster press, so the lobster is a signature dish. Be sure to save room for dessert because Ramsay selected a top-notch and champion pastry chef for his restaurant.\n\n*[Le Pressoir d’Argent](https://www.gordonramsayrestaurants.com/le-pressoir-dargent/), 2-5 Place de la Comedie. Reservations required.*\n\n[![Le Saint-James Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/10/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-France-Bordeaux-Le-Saint-James-6.jpg)\n\nPigeon breast with lardon wrapped green beans and blackberry cream\n\n### Le Saint-James\n\nLe Saint-James is just a few minutes drive across the Garonne in Boulliac, but it’s definitely worth heading out of the city for. Bordelaise chef Nicholas Magie is known for his innovative spin on Bordelaise cuisine and our meal was both delicious and stunningly plated. {[Read the full review](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/le-saint-james-bordeaux/)}\n\nThe menu changes depending on ingredients available seasonally and everything comes from local producers. Order a la carte or let Chef Magie really wow you with a multi-course tasting menu. Also ask for one of the wine pairings to be Le Saint-James own wine produced from their small onsite vineyard, which is actually the smallest vineyard in Bordeaux.\n\n*[Le Saint-James](http://www.saintjames-bouliac.com/fr/), 3 Place Camille Hostein, Boulliac. Reservations required. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/le-saint-james-bouliac-r4977).*\n\n![The sea garden amuse bouche at INIMA](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20759%20468'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nThe sea garden amuse bouche at INIMA\n\n### INIMA\n\nINIMA, which means “with all my heart” in Moldovan, is the creative fine dining restaurant by Moldovan chef Oxana Crétu. Chef Oxana Crétu is the only Moldovan chef in the world to be listed in the Michelin Guide. You can expect an [intimate and elegant dining experience at INIMA](https://bordeauxtravelguide.com/inima-fine-dining-bordeaux/) of which every detail reflects the personality of the former product designer turned gastronomic chef.\n\nThe menu is a tasting menu only with a selection of 6, 8 or 10 courses. Chef Oxana’s signatures are smoked food, and you’ll often find bold and unexpected flavor combinations. Primarily the dishes are fish and seafood based, with the flavors inspired by Oxana’s travels. Ingredients are sourced locally and seasonally from the Nouvelle Aquitaine.\n\nWe tried the 8 course Menu Lily – Rose with stand out dishes such as the sea garden amuse bouche, blue lobster with tomato and vanilla starter, and the duck from the Landes figs leavinng us dreaming of returning to INIMA.\n\nWine pairings are also available, and of particular note was the Fáutor Winery Aurore – a wine from Moldova that paired perfectly with both the duck and red mullet main courses.\n\n*[INIMA](https://inimarestaurant.com/), 48 Rue du Palais Gallien. Reservations required. Book your reservation on the restaurant’s website.*\n\n## For the Best Quick Bites around Bordeaux\n\n![The pulled lamb plate with pickled vegetables and mashed potatoes and the pulled beef a la Bordelaise plate with roast baby potatoes at Filoche](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20630%20750'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nFiloche specializes in slow cooked pulled meats like lamb and beef a la Bordealaise\n\n### Filoche\n\nFiloche is a small, but excellent option for grabbing a quick meal while out and about in Bordeaux. Located just steps away from Porte Cailhau and Place du Palais, [Filoche](https://bordeauxtravelguide.com/filoche-bordeaux/) has a simple menu of the ultimate comfort food: slow cooked pulled meats.\n\nThe idea is simple: slow cooked beef, pork or lamb served either as a sandwich in a brioche roll (think like a lobster roll, but with pulled meat instead) or as a plate served with creamy mashed potatoes or roasted baby potatoes. As the meats cook for 10 hours or more to achieve the incredibly tender meat that melts in your mouth, everything is already prepared before Filoche opens their doors for their lunch and dinner service. That makes for a quick, but gastronomic meal perfect for a quick break while out on a day of [sightseeing around Bordeaux](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/top-things-to-do-in-bordeaux-france/).\n\nOur personal favorite at Filoche is the boeuf à la bordelaise, which reminds us of a rich boeuf bourguignon – just without the carrots and onions, and of course, cooked instead with Bordeaux wine. It goes perfectly with the mashed potatoes.\n\nAs the restaurant is small with just 14 seats, you can also take-away the sandwich version and do as the Bordelais do: enjoy lunch along the quais along the Garonne.\n\n*[Filoche](https://filoche-bordeaux.fr/), 32 Rue des Argentiers*.\n\n![Beef bulgolgi foccacia and kimchi grilled cheese: seasonal sandwiches at Eriu Deli](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20655'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nBeef bulgolgi foccacia and kimchi grilled cheese: seasonal sandwiches at Eriu Deli\n\n### Eriu Deli\n\nEriu Deli is an artisianal sandwich shop, created by owner Grace Lohan of [Cafe Eriu](https://www.cafeeriu.com/), and located a short walk from Jardin Public. Emphasizing local products, Eriu Deli offers a creative and regularly changing menu of delicious sandwiches.\n\nEriu Deli’s menu formula gives you a sandwich of your choice, a drink and either a cookie or pastry like a slice of the famoud Eriu Cafe carrot cake.\n\nThe sandwich shop and epicure is small and is really meant for take-away, though there are a few tables if you really want to eat in. We recommend grabbing a menu formula to go and walking the short distance to enjoy lunch in Jardin Public. Jardin Public is Bordeaux’s main park located right in the urban center, and enjoyed year round by locals and tourists alike. There are plenty of benches along the paths, or do as the locals do and sit in the grass.\n\n*Eriu Deli, 8 Place Charles-Gruet. You can also [order online](https://mylightspeed.app/CYDSXREH/C-ordering/menu) for your order to be ready at a specific pick-up time.*\n\n## For the Best Italian in Bordeaux and the Best Pizza in Bordeaux\n\n![The chef finishes an antipasta plancha at Delizia da Bartolo](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20500'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nDelizia da Bartolo offers a menu of Italian specialties from the Amalfi Coast\n\n### Delizia da Bartolo\n\nA new address in Bordeaux since 2022, Delizia da Bartolo is the fourth establishment by the Neapolitan living in Bordeaux, Bartolo Russo. Delizia da Bartolo, located on the increasingly chic Rue Palais Gallien, offers a menu of specialties of the Amalfi Coast and Neapolitan-style pizza.\n\nStepping in to the restaurant, we were immediately transported back to our years living in Italy. The restaurant is bright and airy with the traditional pottery of the Amalfi Coast adorning both the walls and the tables. One sip of a perfectly concoted Aperol spritz, and I could practically feel the sea breeze.\n\nFresh products are all prepared right in house with ingredients sourced from Italy. The menu is a delight of seafood like the octopus carpaccio (some of the best I’ve had outside of Italy), flavorful pastas and wood-fired Neapolitan pizza.\n\n*[Delizia da Bartolo,](https://www.dabartolo.fr/) 54 Rue du Palais Gallien. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.com/restaurant/la-delizia-da-bartolo-r722305).*\n\n[![Oven baked lasagna at Peppone](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Peppone-Bordeaux-2.jpg)\n\nPeppone’s baked lasagna is one of our favorites\n\n### Peppone\n\nPeppone was our neighbor and the scents of pizza and pizza oven-baked lasagna that wafted in our window daily was enough to make us drool. This Grands Hommes neighborhood restaurant is perpetually busy with both locals and tourists. They don’t take reservations, so the line starts forming a good 30 minutes before their lunch and dinner openings.\n\nThe menu features dishes from all over Italy, and it’s entirely authentic. They even travel to Italy regularly and bring back all their ingredients and the pasta is handmade. You can see wheels of Parmigiano-Reggiano and legs of prosciutto. Wander the giant wine cellar to make your selection, including many of our of Italian favorites.\n\n*[Peppone](https://groupe-peppone.fr/), 31 Georges Clemenceau. Dog-friendly. Reservations not accepted.*\n\n### Bocce\n\nBocce is serious when it comes to Neopolitan-style pizza. They’ve imported their pizza ovens from Naples, Italy. The dough is made with organic flour and worked according to all the Neopolitan rules and tradition. Even all their ingredients are 100% Italian DOP or Presidio Slow Food certified.\n\nAs we lived in Italy for seven years before moving to France, we know authentic Italian pizza and top-quality ingredients. Bocce’s two Bordeaux locations deliver on both, making Bocce one of our go-tos when it comes to a pizza craving.\n\n*[Bocce](http://bocce.fr/), 45 Rue Lachassaigne or 31 Rue Bouquière. Dog-friendly. Reservations recommended.*\n\n[![La Mama Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Bordeaux-La-Mama-1.jpg)\n\nA pizza with chorizo and peppers\n\n### La Mama\n\nLa Mama is totally unpretentious and the paper-topped tables remind us of restaurants in Italy. It’s been a little slice of Italy in Bordeaux since the ’70s. They specialize in pizzas, though do have several other Italian specialties on their menu. The wine list isn’t very extensive, but unlike Peppone they do take reservations.\n\n*La Mama, 21 Rue des Remparts. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/la-mama-r79094).*\n\n[![Half and half Neopolitan pizza at Restaurant Pizzeria Masaniello](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Restaurant-Pizzeria-Masaniello-Bordeaux-2.jpg)\n\nMasaniello specializes in Neopolitan pizza\n\n### Restaurant Pizzeria Masaniello\n\nThe three brothers Salvatore, Alessandro and Davide hail from Naples and brought their Neopolitan pizza with them here to Bordeaux. Their members of the Associazione Verace Pizza Napoletana, a non-profit that was founded in Naples in 1984 to ensure the authenticity of Neopolitan pizza, so you can be assured you’re getting the real deal.\n\nAside from the Neopolitan pizzas that Masaniello specializes in, you’ll find pastas and other Neopolitan dishes made from fresh and seasonal ingredients straight from Naples. We highly recommend the pizza. They’ll even do a half and half pizza since the hardest thing about Masaniello is selecting which pizza you want – and you’ll want them all with ingredients like mozzarella bufala straight from the buffalo farms in Campania.\n\n*Restaurant Pizzeria Masaniello, 10 Rue Maucoudinat. Dog-friendly. Reservations recommended.*\n\n## For the Best Burger Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\n![The M truffle smash and a side of fries at Junk Smash Burgers](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20563%20750'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nThe M truffle smash at Junk Smash Burgers\n\n### Junk Smash Burgers\n\nWhen it comes to smash burgers, Junk Smash Burgers is without a doubt the [best burger in Bordeaux](https://bordeauxtravelguide.com/best-burgers-in-bordeaux/).\n\nThe formula at Junk is simple. The smash burgers come in sizes S – XXL with 1 – 5 perfectly caramelized smash patties made from Charolais and Limousin (French) beef on a home baked buttery French brioche bun and finished with cheese and Junk’s secret sauce. Add jalapeños for an extra kick or get indulgent with their truffle smash burger.\n\n*[Junk Bordeaux](https://www.junkburgers.com/), 7 Rue Judaïque.* *Dog-friendly. Dine in, take-away and delivery available.*\n\n### Edmond Pure Burger\n\nAs Americans, we know a good burger. And while just about every bistrot offers a burger and there’s seemingly a burger joint on just about every corner, we find it’s actually few and far between that have a truly good burger.\n\nEdmond Pure Burger tops our list of the truly [best burgers in Bordeaux](https://bordeauxtravelguide.com/best-burgers-in-bordeaux/). Family-run with two Bordeaux locations, Edmond specializes in burgers. Their buns are baked twice daily right there in house, the beef is sourced only from France and the cheese comes from locally.\n\n**Edmond Pure Burger, 34 Rue Palais Gallien and 158 Cours Victor Hugo.** *Dog-friendly. Dine in, take-away and delivery available.*\n\n## For the Best International Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\n[![Le Bordeaux Gordon Ramsay](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/08/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Intercontintental-Bordeaux-Le-Bordeaux-Gordon-Ramsay-1.jpg)\n\nBeef wellington at Le Bordeaux Gordon Ramsay\n\n### Le Bordeaux Gordon Ramsay\n\nLe Bordeaux Gordon Ramsay is Ramsay’s second restaurant in Bordeaux, this one serving up a menu with a mix of British and French fare. We’ve long been fans of Ramsay’s beef wellington and after years of watching him on *Hell’s Kitchen*, we just had to try it at the now sadly closed Gordon Ramsay at Claridge’s in London. We’ve seen had this dish at some of Ramsay’s other restaurants like Gordon Ramsay Steak in the Paris Las Vegas and now we’re thrilled it’s on the menu at Le Bordeaux Gordon Ramsay.\n\nAside from this obviously personal favorite, the bistro is set on the best terrace in Bordeaux on Place de la Comédie. I’m convinced if you sit there long enough, every tourist in Bordeaux that day will pass by. It is definitely one of my favorite people watching spots and the little fireplace heaters make it possible to comfortably enjoy the terrace even on Bordeaux’s coldest days.\n\nOther bistro favorites are the seasonal soups (I’m totally waiting for summer and the strawberry gazpacho to hopefully make a reappearance!) and the burgers.\n\n*[Le Bordeaux Gordon Ramsay](https://bordeaux.intercontinental.com/en/le-bordeaux/), 2 Place de la Comédie. Reservations recommended.*\n\n![An Argentian steak with fries at El Nacional in Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nEl Nacional serves the best steak frites in Bordeaux\n\n### El Nacional – Best Steakhouse in Bordeaux\n\nIf you want to find the best steak frites in Bordeaux without a wait, skip the massive queue that forms outside of L’Entrecôte and instead head to a gem loved by locals in the Chartrons neighborhood. El Nacional is an Argentinean steak house in the clásico bodegón porteño style you’ll find in Argentina. It’s known for its meats and is popular with locals, particularly to bring someone for a business dinner.\n\nThis restaurant is all about the meat, and the menu features both Argentian and French cuts of beef cooked on the plancha. The wine list has 200 references, with a good selection of both Argentinian and Bordeaux wines. Come hungry, because you certainly won’t leave that way.\n\n*[El Nacional](https://www.elnacional.fr/), 23 Rue Rode*. *Reservations recommended.*\n\n![A beef and halloumi souvlaki wrap and chicken souvalki wrap with sides of spicy baby potatoes and Coco the Traveling Samoyed hoping for a bite at Kalimera Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20563%20750'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nSouvlaki wraps at Kalimera\n\n### Kalimera – Best Greek Street Food in Bordeaux\n\nKalimera serves up Greek street food straight from Athens, where Kalimera founder Télémanque Argyriou is from.\n\nKalimera made its debut with a food truck in London’s Hoxton Street Market in 2015, before expanding with brick and mortar locations in London, Paris, Lille and now Bordeaux. The famous souvlaki wraps even are a staple at Wimbledon.\n\nWe tested the Bordeaux Kalimera location and tried the pistachio feta saganáki topped with hot honey and served with spicy houmous. It’s one of several mezze you can choose from on the menu to share and the one we highly recommend. Each bite was like being transported back to eating my way through the Monastiraki District in Athens.\n\nEmblematic of Greek street food, Kalimera’s souvlaki wraps are not to be missed. Choose from chicken, beef or halloumi with a generous side of either spicy baby potatoes topped with kefaltoyari cheese or chickpea and sun-dried tomato salad.\n\nYou can even pair souvlaki or moussaka with a selection of wines from Greece and Macedonia on the menu.\n\n*[Kalimera](https://www.eatkalimera.com/), 1 Place Marie de Gournay. Dog-friendly. No reservations necessary, but recommended.*\n\n![Slow roasted beef arepa and build your own tacos at Macondo Café](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nSlow roasted beef arepa and build your own tacos at Macondo Café\n\n### Macondo Café\n\nFamily-run by the Prado family originally from Colombia, Macondo Café started as a food truck back in 2019. We discovered it at Bordeaux Fête Le Vin and sought them out for their delicious arepas ever since!\n\nNow Macondo has opened a dog-friendly restaurant in Bordeaux Centre. You’ll not only find exhibitions of Latin American art like the Colombian hats and posters that adorn the walls, but authentic cuisine emblematic of the region.\n\nWhile the menu changes seasonally, currently there are delicious empanadas made the Colombian way with corn dough and guacamole served with mini arepas as starters; arepas stuffed with slow simmered beef or chicken, marinated pollo al coco or melting cactus leaves as mains; and a Brazilian coffee torta for dessert.\n\n*[Macondo Café](https://www.macondobordeaux.com/), 25 Rue du Dr Charles Nancel Penard*. *Dog-friendly. Reservations highly recommended. Book your reservation on their website.*\n\n## For the Best Asian Restaurants in Bordeaux\n\n[![Madame Pang Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20664'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Best-Bordeaux-Restaurants-Madame-Pang-1.jpg)\n\nPeking Ravioli stuffed with free range chicken and topped with an almost crusty melted parmesan\n\n### Madame Pang\n\nMadame Pang is a still fairly new glamorous dim sum bar that opened on Rue de la Devise, steps away from Place de la Bourse in the heart of Bordeaux. They don’t take reservations for dinner and if you’re not there right around when it opens, a line for one of the small tables with velvet chairs forms. But unlike the ridiculous always present line wrapping around the block at L’Entrecôte, a wait for a table at Madame Pang is oh-so-worth it.\n\nThe dim sum bar is the vision of Jérôme and Harmony Billot, a Chinese-Savoyard couple, and the heritage ensures a delicious fusion of French meets Cantonese and Hong Kong. The small bites are prepared and served up in the traditional bamboo baskets. We suggest starting with 3-4 selections per couple, then ordering more if you’re still hungry. For dinner, we selected 6 menu items in all and it was more than enough for the two of us.\n\nIt can be hard to decide with 14 different dim sum, plus other small plates and finger foods to choose from. We highly recommend the Peking Ravioli, a ravioli stuffed with a juicy free range chicken and topped with parmesan and chives. Not quite what we were expecting, but a definitive favorite is the Xiao Long Bao from the rare dim sum menu. It’s a steamed bun filled with pork and jellied broth, then topped with a truffle coulis that almost looks like chocolate. Certainly not for everyone, but if you’re feeling a bit adventurous, the duck hearts with black vinegar are melt-in-your-mouth good. And the sweet and sour pork is flavorful, but not too sweet.\n\nOf course, you can’t come to Madame Pang and not try their selection of cocktails. They also have a good selection of Asian beers, and as this is Bordeaux, a nice wine list.\n\n*[Madame Pang](http://www.madamepang.com/), 16 Rue de la Devise. Reservations not accepted.*\n\n![Pork belly at OBA](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20619%20750'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nPork belly at OBA\n\n### OBA\n\nOBA is a bistronomic izakaya blending French and Japanese cultures throuogh seasonal small plates meant to be shared among friends.\n\nFitting as OBA is the brain child of two friends: Chloé Cazaux Grandpierre, a renowned sake sommelier, and Patrick Herreyre, founder of the restaurant Nama Wine Restaurant (included below). The concept is their French take on the Japanese izakaya with a short menu featuring seasonal and ultra fresh ingredients in a variety of small plates meant to be shared. Choose the menu Omakase for 5 dishes, and let your palate be delighted with sakes to pair.\n\nOBA is intimate with just 20 seats, so be sure to reserve in advance for this popular Bordeaux Japanese bistro.\n\n*[OBA](https://www.obabordeaux.com/), 15 Rue Emile Duployé. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly.*\n\n[![Mixed sushi plate at Sushi DOZO](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Sushi-Dozo-Bordeaux-1.jpg)\n\nMixed sushi plate at Sushi DOZO\n\n### Sushi DOZO\n\nSushi DOZO isn’t a spot a tourist will just stumble upon. It’s just a few minutes walk from Place Gambetta, but tucked away from the sights. But it’s definitely worth seeking out for delicious sushi, refined setting and reasonable (for sushi in France) prices.\n\nGo for lunch when they have a lunch menu with a variety of different rolls and sushi on the plate. One mixed lunch plate and a dragon roll was plenty for us to share, but for us to still have room to try the green tea matcha cheesecake for dessert.\n\nYou can either sit at the counter, where you can watch the sushi chefs prepare the rolls, or at one of the handful of tables. The ingredients used are clearly high quality and this is a good choice for sushi in Bordeaux.\n\n*[Sushi DOZO](http://www.sushidozo.fr/), 36 Rue du Dr Charles Nancel Penard*\n\n![A flatlay of the table with beef bulgogi, Korean kimchi beef tacos, kimchi nachos and dak gang jung at Bibibap Korean restaurant in Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nBibibap serves Korean classics along with the chef’s unique Korean-inspired creations like kimchi beef tacos\n\n### Bibibap\n\nWe’ve tried a few Korean restaurants around Bordeaux, but they just never quite measured up. Tim spent two different stints living in South Korea and knows what good Korea food is. Bibibap on Place Camile Julian is now our go to for Korean food.\n\nTheir house dish is bibimbap, a classic Korean dish that quite literally means rice mixture. But their chef has created some other Korean tapas unique to Bibibap, incorporating kimchi, the national dish of Korea into his creations. Our favorite are the kimchi and beef tacos topped with a spicy mayonnaise. The Korean tacos are on the “sides” menu, but the portion of two tacos overflowing with thinly sliced bulgogi-style beef are filling, especially when paired with another side like the perfectly fried, spicy-sweet dak gang jung.\n\n*[Bibibap](https://www.bibibap.fr/), 46 Pas-Saint-Georges. Dog-friendly.*\n\n### Mokoji Grill\n\nAs I mentioned, Tim lived in South Korea. One thing he’d always rave about is Korean BBQ. These restaurants have a grill right at your table where your meat is cooked with fresh vegetables and mushrooms right in front of you.\n\nTo our surprise, there are actually a few Korean BBQ restaurants in Bordeaux. But it is Mokoji Grill in the Saint-Pierre district that we find the most authentic and delicious experience. The menu is simple selecting pork, pork and beef or premium black angus beef. But you most certainly won’t leave hungry.\n\nA selection of various Korean salads and sides arrives while your BBQ grill warms up. Then it’s a flurry of meat and cooking while the wait staff expertly grills up the various meats and vegetables. Have your camera ready!\n\nA little more on the expensive end, we like Mokoji Grill for a fun occassion like a birthday or a night out with friends.\n\n*Mokoji Grill, 45 Rue du Pas-Saint-Georges. Reservations recommended.*\n\n![A selection of dishes at NaiNai including bao buns served in a steamer basket, fried eggplant and pork with rice](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20580'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nNaiNai serves authentic Chinese recipes which are a nod to the their homeland\n\n### Naï Naï\n\nFor authentic Chinese dishes, head to the new Naï Naï located just steps from Place Gambetta. Owners Romain and his wife Gao Lin come from Wenzhou and Tianjin, bringing with them delicious authentic recipes. The menu is inspired by and a nod to Gao Lin’s grandmother’s recipes.\n\nYou’ll find Gao Lin and their staff in the open chef’s kitchen cooking up homemade bao buns, their signature braised barbecue pork and a small menu of other dishes the couple fondly recalls from their childhood in China.\n\nNaï Naï doesn’t accept reservations, and since locals caught on to the light and delicious cuisine served up here, the 30-seat dining room often quickly fills up. So arrive a few minutes before opening to ensure you get a table.\n\n*[Naï Naï](https://nainai.fr/), 23 Ruee du Dr Charles Nancel Penard. Reservations not accepted.*\n\n[![A bowl of pho and a bowl of Tom Yum Goong with plates of herbs and sprouts at Panya Thip Eurasie](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Eurasie-Bordeaux-Lac-2.jpg)\n\nA bowl of pho and a bowl of Tom Yum Goong with plates of herbs and sprouts to season it\n\n### Panya Thip at Eurasie\n\nNo frills, but absolutely delicious. Eurasie is Bordeaux’s Asian supermarket and now there are actually a couple of locations. The original and the one closest to Bordeaux Center is Eurasie Bordeaux Lac, where you’ll find a no frills deli-style cafeteria. There’s a long counter with countless selection of dim sum, there’s a hot plates stall serving a couple plates of the day with an ever changing menu, and then there’s the little soup stand with a line usually stretching out the door. It’s Panya Thip and their specialty is their authentic Vietnamese pho. Panya Thip also usually has one other soup to choose from, which changes often, like Tom Yum Goong. All their recipes are family recipes and the “soup stall” is family run.\n\nThe portion is plentiful and I usually don’t finish it. So as tempting as all the dim sum is, we suggest passing it up if you’re going for a bowl of pho. It comes will all the fixings to add as much herbs and sprouts as you personally like. Just take the tray over to one of the cafeteria tables in the small seating area to enjoy. You can also bring your own container to take pho to go.\n\n*[Panyaa Thip Eurasie](https://www.eurasie-bordeaux.com/panya-thip), 10 Avenue de Tourville. Reservations not accepted. Dog-friendly on the outdoor terrace. Cash and credit/debit card accepted.*\n\n![Gyozas with shitake mushroom and edamame at Nama](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20623'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nAdventurous diners will like the surprise menu, like this starter of prok & shrimp gyozas with shiitake mushroom and edamame at Name\n\n### Nama Wine Restaurant\n\nNama is the creation of two chefs, one French and one Japanese. The two cultures collide in delicious Franco-Japanese cuisine. The name “Nama” is Japanese for fresh and authentic, and the regularly changing menu reflects exactly that. The menu showcases fresh ingredients from local producers such as Tauziet & Co Farm, who supplies to some of our favorite Bordeaux restaurateurs and artisan producers.\n\nChoose from the a la carte menu or leave it up to the chefs with a surprise menu including starter, main course and dessert. You’ll find rich dishes like the pork and shrimp filled gyozas cooked in miso with shiitake mushroom, black garlic, white radish and edamame we had most recently at Nama.\n\nYou can also expect excellent wine pairing recommendations with the dishes and even blind tastings, as these chefs are also passionate wine connoisseurs.\n\n*[Nama Wine Restaurant](http://www.namawinerestaurant.com/), 24 Rue Lafaurie de Monbadon. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/nama-r63580).*\n\n## For the Best Food Halls in Bordeaux\n\n## For the Best Wine Bars in Bordeaux\n\nWine bars are just about everywhere in Bordeaux. But often just as with restaurants in France, it can be impossible to get a table without a reservation. Thursday nights, in particular, are *the* going out on the town night in Bordeaux for locals.\n\nWine bars can range from everything from upscale establishments where you can both taste and buy wine to rowdy local favorites where a rugby match is always on a tv hung above the bar. The best wine bar really depends on the type of night our you’re looking for.\n\nIt’s also good to know that even in the World Capital of Wine, obtaining a liquor license is no easy feat. Most wine bars have the type of license in which the customer is required to purchase a food item in order to also order a glass of wine, a cocktail or a beer. The food offered is typically various charcuterie boards that are ample enough to be a meal for two. If you’re just in it for the glass of wine, some wine bars offer small tapas for just €1-2 – though these small items might not always be listed on the menu, so ask.\n\nThere’s also something typically for the non-drinker and a number of wine bars also offer juice or soda.\n\n![Coco the Traveling Samoyed poses at Le Bar à Vin with two glasses of white bordeaux and a cheese plate](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20562%20750'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nLe Bar à Vin offers some of the best priced wine by-the-glass in Bordeaux, and it’s a dog-friendly wine bar\n\n### Le Bar à Vin (CIVB)\n\nA Bordeaux institution, Le Bar à Vin is loved by many locals and you’ll often hear them refer to it at the CIVB. That’s because it’s run by the Conseil Interprofessionnel du Vin de Bordeaux, which is the orginizaiton responsible for representing the wine appellations of Bordeaux. And that’s precisely why I personally love Le Bar à Vin.\n\nYou’ll find some of the best priced by-the-glass wine here, along with a small menu of cheese and charcuterie plates to snack on. The wines on offer change regularly, with the CIVB showcasing the various 65 appellations of the Bordeaux wine region.\n\n*Le Bar à Vin, 3 Cours du Juillet. Reservations not accepted. Dog-friendly.*\n\n![The gourmand tasting with savory waffle, spinach soup, salmon tartar, and curry with wine pairings and the menu in a book ](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20535'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nLes très gourmand: a tasting of 5 gourmet bites at Les Doux Secrets d’Hélène\n\n### Les Doux Secretes d’Hélène\n\nTucked away on a quiet and otherwise residential street, Les Doux Secrets d’Hélène is steps away from the Grosse Cloche. If you didn’t know about this cozy wine bar, you’d likely not discover it on your own. Thankfully, we’ve done the research for you and Les Doux Secrets d’Hélène is definitely a destination worth seeking out.\n\nNamed for its’ passionate owner, Hélène Orhon, it’s a place that feels like you’ve been invited over to Hélène’s home for some wine and delicious nibbles. She’s a sommelier and knowledgeable about all 300 references she carries. Don’t hesitate to ask for her suggestions for the best wine she has at the moment. On our recent visit, it just happened to be a red from Italy’s Umbria region.\n\nHélène offers a tasting menu of five gourmet bites, which she changes weekly to reflect the season’s local products available. It’s called ‘les très gourmand’ and we recommend going with this option.\n\nThere’s also a Sunday brunch from 11:30am – 2:30pm for €27 per person.\n\n*[Les Doux Secrets d’Hélène](http://www.lesdouxsecretsdhelene.fr/), 41 Rue Neuve. Reservations recommended. Dog-friendly. Book your reservation on [TheFork](https://www.thefork.fr/restaurant/les-doux-secrets-d-helene-r380041).*\n\n## For the Best Brunch in Bordeaux\n\n![Suzzi Kafe bruch with Swedish waffles, yogurt with granola, potato pancakes, and open-faced Swedish sandwich](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nSuzzi Kafe offers a Nordic style brunch every day\n\n### Suzzi Kafe\n\nSuzzi Kafe is a little taste of Nordic cocooning right in the heart of Bordeaux just steps away from the Cathédrale Saint André. The dream of two travel friends who met in Australia, Suzzi is the creation of Margaux (French) and Arvid (Swedish). Unique in Bordeaux, the cafe is a tribute to Arvid’s home country of Sweden and he makes everything homemade.\n\nSuzzi offers brunch every day (Wednesday through Sunday, since Suzzi is closed on Monday and Tuesday). The brunch forumla is fantastic, and it certainly won’t leave you hungry. Included in the €22 brunch formula you have your choice of a smörgås open-faced sandwich, potato pancakes with either bacon and ligonberry jam or veggie, and either their gourmet Swedish waffle with your choice of toppings or Suzzi’s famous cinnamon roll. Brunch also comes with yogurt with their homemade granola, fresh pressed orange juice and either coffee or tea.\n\nSuzzi’s brunch is the perfect blend of savory and sweet, and you’ll definitely be satisfied from this feast all day. Believe me, it’s worth the wait for a table since reservations are not accepted.\n\n*[Suzzi Kafe](https://suzzikafe.fr/), 46 Rue des Trois-Conils*. *Reservations not accepted.*\n\n![Our table with the hot plate with scrambled eggs on an english muffin, sausage, beans and a grilled tomato at La Grande Poste](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nLa Grande Poste is known for one of the best brunches in Bordeaux\n\n### La Grande Poste\n\nLa Grande Poste is a beautiful Art Deco building in Bordeaux’s hyper centre that formally served as the main Post Office of Bordeaux. The Sunday brunch is regularly listed among the best brunches in Bordeaux.\n\nEach weekend there’s a different band that plays live during the brunch beginning at 1pm. And La Grande Poste has two menus you can choose from: one with a hot plate plus the buffet or just the buffet.\n\nHonestly, the buffet alone would have been plenty. The buffet includes many different options such as mini charcuterie plates, mini cheese plates, full croque monsieurs, salads, fruit, pastries and more. There’s also a variety of juices available and coffees including cappuccino are served from the bar. If you do opt for the hot plate, there is one selection prepared for the week’s theme.\n\n*[La Grande Poste](http://lagrandeposte.com/), 7 Rue du Palais Gallien. Reservations required as the brunch books up.*\n\n![Brakfast tacos topped with eggs and American pancakes with fried chicken tenders and beer at The Frog & Rosbif Bordeaux](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20562'%3E%3C/svg%3E)\n\nThe Frog & Rosbif Bordeaux offers an American-style Sunday brunch with craft beer\n\n### The Frog & Rosbif Bordeaux\n\nThe Frog & Rosbif is a micro-brewery housed in a 16th century historic building that was formerly a women’s prison. It’s the first brew pub to brew their own beers right here in the historic center of Bordeaux. On Sundays, the micro-brewery offers a delicious Sunday brunch with a selection of American-style breakfast dishes served up with a pint of their latest on-tap beers.\n\nTry the breakfast tacos filled with layers of pico de gallo, guacamole, BBQ pulled pork, bacon and topped with fried eggs, which is our favorite of the breakfast options. The American-style pancakes are a short stack of fluffy pancakes layered with fried chicken tenders and slices of bacon, then drizzled with maple syrup. Everything comes with a side of fries, a pint of beer (or a glass of wine, juice, or a soft drink) and coffee, tea or hot chocolate.\n\n*[The Frog & Rosbif Bordeaux](https://www.frogpubs.com/pub-the-frog-rosbif-bordeaux-bordeaux-7.php), 23 Rue Ausone. Reservations required.* *Dog-friendly.*\n\n## For the Best Ptisserie in Bordeaux\n\n[![Cream puff dusted with powdered sugar](data:image/svg+xml,%3Csvg%20xmlns='http://www.w3.org/2000/svg'%20viewBox='0%200%20750%20563'%3E%3C/svg%3E)](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/wp-content/uploads/2017/01/Luxe-Adventure-Traveler-Dunes-Blanches-Chez-Pascal-Bordeaux-1.jpg)\n\nDunes Blanches are the best cream puffs in the world\n\n### Dunes Blanches chez Pascal\n\nDunes Blanches chez Pascal sell one thing, and one thing only: their pastries named Dunes Blanches.\n\nThe pastries, which are a type of cream puff filled with chantilly cream and then dusted with powdered sugar and candied bits of sugar, are named for the white sand dunes that surround the Bassin of Arcachon. It was in the Bassin that Pascal has his first pastry shop and these bites of heaven were invented. As the story goes, Pascal’s son came home having had a little too much wine early one morning. Pascal was already making the pastry known as chouquettes for the day, and his son grabbed the chantilly cream and filled a chouquette with it. Whether the story is true, we’ll never know for sure. But if it is, we have Pascal’s son’s druken escapades to thank.\n\nYou can get the Dunes Blanches by the piece. There’s no tables to sit and enjoy them, but you can take them around the corner to the Place Gambetta and enjoy them in the park like many Bordelaise do. Or [L’Alchimiste](http://alchimiste-cafes.com/) is a wonderful coffee shop kitty-corner from the patstry shop and just happen to sell Pascal’s dunes blanches as well. These are the only two places you can get them in all of Bordeaux.\n\n*[Dunes Blanches chez Pascal](http://boulangerie-chezpascal.com/), 7 Rue de la Vieille Tour*\n\n*We’ve been invited with complimentary meals at some restaurants on this list in order to bring you this story. However, Luxe Adventure Traveler maintains full editorial control of the content published on this site. As always, all thoughts, opinions, and enthusiasm for travel are entirely our own.*\n\nLoved this post? We love when you share our content! ~ Jen & Coco\n\n* [Share](https://www.facebook.com/sharer/sharer.php?u=https%3A%2F%2Fbit.ly%2F2WiMJ7j&t=A%20Local%27s%20Take%20on%20the%20Best%20Restaurants%20in%20Bordeaux%20-%20Luxe%20Adventure%20Traveler \"Share on Facebook\")\n* [Tweet](https://x.com/intent/tweet?text=A%20Local%27s%20Take%20on%20the%20Best%20Restaurants%20in%20Bordeaux%20-%20Luxe%20Adventure%20Traveler&url=https%3A%2F%2Fbit.ly%2F2WiMJ7j&via=jdomb \"Share on X\")\n* [Buffer](https://buffer.com/add?url=https%3A%2F%2Fbit.ly%2F2WiMJ7j&text=A%20Local%27s%20Take%20on%20the%20Best%20Restaurants%20in%20Bordeaux%20-%20Luxe%20Adventure%20Traveler \"Share on Buffer\")\n* [Flipboard](https://share.flipboard.com/bookmarklet/popout?v=2&url=https%3A%2F%2Fbit.ly%2F2WiMJ7j&title=A%20Local%27s%20Take%20on%20the%20Best%20Restaurants%20in%20Bordeaux%20-%20Luxe%20Adventure%20Traveler \"Share on Flipboard\")\n* [Reddit](https://www.reddit.com/submit?url=https%3A%2F%2Fbit.ly%2F2WiMJ7j&title=A%20Local%27s%20Take%20on%20the%20Best%20Restaurants%20in%20Bordeaux%20-%20Luxe%20Adventure%20Traveler \"Share on Reddit\")\n* [Threads](https://www.threads.net/intent/post?text=A%20Local%27s%20Take%20on%20the%20Best%20Restaurants%20in%20Bordeaux%20-%20Luxe%20Adventure%20Traveler+https%3A%2F%2Fbit.ly%2F2WiMJ7j \"Share on Threads\")\n\n#### About Jennifer Dombrowski\n\nJennifer Dombrowski is an independent travel publisher and an American expat who has lived in Bordeaux, France since 2016. She previously lived in Northern Italy in a small village near Venice for seven years where she fell in love with wine and wine tourism. She is an award-winning travel writer. She was also a travel correspondent on Traveling on the American Forces Radio Network. Luxe Adventure Traveler was named one of the top travel blogs to watch by the Huffington Post and TripAdvisor, and has been featured by top publications such as National Geographic, CNN, Buzzfeed, and Business Insider. Jennifer's photography has also been featured on publications such as USA Today and Travel + Leisure and on the Travel Channel.\n\n### Comments\n\n1. Gokul Raj says\n\n   Really nice pics. Bella says\n\n    It seems that Bordeaux has absolutely no shortage of good restaurants. Your photos are spectacular, and the food looks delicious. We’re not fans of Kokomo. We find the burgers are small and just okay. Agree that Edmond Pure Burger is good and also like Guy & Sons.\n\n      [Reply](#comment-521135)\n25. Jodie Mortimer says\n\n    Hi there,  \n     Heading to the area later next week and wanted to visit the centre of Bordeaux on Friday. Can you recommend somewhere really good for lunch with outside seating and that would also be suitable for a toddler?  \n     Many thanks  \n     Jodie\n\n    [Reply](#comment-521545)\n\n    * [Jennifer Dombrowski](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com) says\n\n      Hi Jodie,\n\n      Most restaurants, unless touristic, do not offer kids menus and rarely have a high chair available. French kids eat the same food as adults. So with that in mind as far as the toddler, any restaurant is suitable really.\n\n      It’s also going to be quite hot next week with the temps predicted to go up to around 90F. So you may want to seek out a place out of the sun for a bit of a reprieve from the heat. But the restaurants that have outdoor seating that we recommend are La Tupina, Le Chien de Pavlov, Symbiose, Bistro du Musée and Mampuku. We also just tried an Italian restaurant that is fantastic and we’ll be adding to our recommendations: Masaniello. They also have outdoor seating. Any of these are all really good.\n\n      [Reply](#comment-521546)\n26. Jo says\n\n    Hi there,\n\n    Can you recommend any restaurants in Bordeaux that will have a courtyard for dining? We are hoping to travel to Bordeaux to celebrate my birthday and looking for a suitable restaurant that could cater for 15-20 people, but preferably with outdoor seating (that is not on the street).\n\n    Kind regards,  \n     Jo\n\n    [Reply](#comment-522205)\n27. Lili says\n\n    Hi,Jennifer and Tim. I’m a tea sommelier and heading to Bordeaux in early June. I love “tea” , any suggestions as where to find the best “tea and food pairings in Bordeaux?  \n     Cheers\n\n    [Reply](#comment-522472)\n\n    * [Jennifer Dombrowski](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com) says\n\n      Hi Lili,\n\n      I also love tea! I’m sad to tell you that tea is not a big thing in French culture. There are tea salons, and typically tea is drunk with a pastry. Even at Le Grand Hotel, which offers an afternoon tea, it is served only with sweets and not also the savories like a typical afternoon tea you’d go to in the US or England. You won’t find any tea and food pairing workshops in Bordeaux. La Cité du Vin offered a special workshop with tea once, but I don’t think it was very popular because I’ve never seen it on the calendar of events ever again.\n\n      As far as tea, you might like to visit some of the tea salons. I can also recommend a visit to Chris’Teas. It’s an independent tea shop and the owner, Christel, blends her own teas. She doesn’t do any food pairing workshops, but you’ll probably enjoy the shop!\n\n      [Reply](#comment-522473)\n28. Koby says\n\n    Hi,\n\n    My wife and I are travelling to Bordeaux in about a month. We are looking to make lunch and/or dinner reservations. I’m going through your site and there is a lot to digest. I wanted to see if you had any absolute must go to places we have to eat at to get the best of Bordeaux. Please let me know.\n\n    (We will be using your links to make reservations. I work in affiliate marketing so know how things work)\n\n    Thanks,\n\n    Koby\n\n    [Reply](#comment-522559)\n\n    * [Jennifer Dombrowski](https://luxeadventuretraveler.com) says\n\n      Hi Koby,\n\n      Depending on when you visit Bordeaux in June, the tall ships festival called Fete le Fleuve is here from June 20 – 23. There will be many pop-up restaurants all along the riverfront for the festival as well. You’ll be able to try many things from the region.\n\n      As for restaurants, if we had to just pick a few we would say:\n\n      \\*La Tupina for a dinner. This is a Bordeaux institution and traditional cuisine. Great ambiance with the traditional fire they still use for cooking. It’s a focus on meat, so if you are not a meat eater then I would skip it.  \n       \\*When we have visitors, we always take them for a lunch at Le Chien de Pavlov. The menu changes constantly and is just a chalkboard menu. It’s the perfect little French bistro with good prices.  \n       \\*If you can get a reservation at Symbiose, we highly recommend this restaurant for modern French cuisine and top notch cocktails.  \n       \\*For a splurge and a very nice dinner, Gordon Ramsay’s Le Pressoir d’Argent is truly one of the best of Bordeaux.\n\n      Will you visit any wine chteaux? If so, we really recommend the restaurant La Chapelle at Chteau Guiraud for lunch. It’s traditional Gascony cuisine with a modern twist. We’ve written about it here: <https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/chateau-guiraud-sauternes/>\n\n      And truly one of the very best restaurants of the Bordeaux region is the new Michelin-star Restaurant Lalique at Chteau Lafaurie Peyraguey. Just wow! The food is art. It is most definitely a big splurge to dine here and we recommend dinner when you will experience the sunset. Request a table specifically in the greenhouse. <https://luxeadventuretraveler.com/chateau-lafaurie-peyraguey-sauternes-france/>\n\n      I hope all of that helps narrow things down a bit. Let us know if you have any other questions! We’re always happy to help.\n\n      Enjoy your trip to Bordeaux!\n\n      [Reply](#comment-522560)\n29. Anna says\n\n    Really amazing post and super informative ! I love reading your posts. I needed the ones in Bordeaux and you really helped me since I will be travelling with my husband there.\n\n    [Reply](#comment-522570)\n30. Jordana says\n\n    Hello!       "raw_content": "[![Accor Live Limitless](https://all.accor.com/components/header/assets/icons/brands/all.svg)](https://all.accor.com)\n\n[![Accor Live Limitless](https://all.accor.com/components/header/assets/icons/brands/all.svg)](https://all.accor.com)\n\n![](/fact-sheet/assets/icons/brands/full/color/mer.svg) \n\n# Mercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons Hotel 4 stars\n\nCustomer review rating (ALL Rating) 4.4/5 ![](/fact-sheet/assets/icons/first/gss-rating-45.svg) [1,840 reviews](#section-reviews)\n\n[Best price  \nguarantee](/best-price-guarantee/index.en.shtml \"Best price<br/>guarantee - New Window\")\n\n![Mercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons Hotel - Image 1](https://www.ahstatic.com/photos/1810_ho_00_p_1024x768.jpg)\n\n![Mercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons Hotel - Image 2](https://www.ahstatic.com/photos/1810_ho_01_p_1024x768.jpg)\n\n![Mercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons Hotel - Image 3](https://www.ahstatic.com/photos/1810_ho_02_p_1024x768.jpg)\n\n![Mercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons Hotel - Image 4](https://www.ahstatic.com/photos/1810_ho_03_p_1024x768.jpg)\n\n86   86 photos/video\n\n80   80 photos/video\n\n[81 cours Saint-Louis, 33300 BORDEAUX, France](#section-location)  -\n\n[See rates](#section-rooms)\n\n## Description\n\nMercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons Hotel\n\nHotel for travellers looking for local and authentic experiences\n\nHoused in former renovated cellars, Mercure Bordeaux Chteau Chartrons offers professional and friendly services: restaurant under a glass roof opening onto a patio, cozy bar, garden for sharing cocktails and mezze, fitness center for guests only, 11 meeting rooms with natural lighting, large atrium for seminar breaks. Our 215 travel-themed rooms in various categories invite you to relax during your stay.\n\nThe hotel also has an A320 flight simulator where you can experience a unique immersive activity accompanied by an airline pilot. Experience this unforgettable moment over a weekend or with your colleagues between meetings. Bordeaux and its landscape are a delight you won't soon forget.\n\nThe hotel is the ideal base for exploring the chteaux and vineyards of Bordeaux. You can also head to the Bassin d'Arcachon, the Dune du Pilat or Cap Ferret to sample oysters and admire the ocean view.\n\n> Welcome to Bordeaux's beautiful Chartrons district! Enjoy a typical southwest welcome: professional, authentic and caring. Discover gourmet food and conviviality in the hotel's restaurant and bar.\n\nEmeric Autier, Hotel Management\n\n### Hotel extras\n\n* Secure private car park reserved for hotel guests.\n* \" Le C\" restaurant, tasty cuisine combining local and Mediterranean notes.\n* \"Le C\" garden for sipping cocktails and sharing mezze in the sunshine.\n* \"Le C\" bar for a glass of local wine, open every day
